People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I know that might just sound like normal responsibility, but it feels like it’s taking up a lot of space in my head.\n\nDoctor: Is this worry and anxiety mainly focused on work, or does it also affect other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly work, I think… or that’s where it starts, anyway. Like the launch numbers or whether someone on my team is unhappy, that kind of thing.\n\nBut it doesn’t really stay neatly in that box. It’ll jump to the mortgage, or a client renewal, and then I’m treating some small delay like it’s a sign something bigger is going wrong. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic, but it feels hard to shut off once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or worried even when you're not thinking about work or specific problems like the mortgage?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Maybe not “worried” in a clear, specific way all the time, but I feel kind of wound up even when nothing is actually happening.\n\nLike I can be at home eating dinner, and my body still feels like I’m sitting in a pipeline review or waiting for bad news. My shoulders stay tight a lot, even when I’m trying to relax. I don’t know if that counts as anxiety exactly, but it doesn’t feel like I fully switch off.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling wound up even when trying to relax, does that feeling of being wound up go away at any point during the day?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I could say it does. It might ease up a little if I’m distracted, but even then I still feel kind of wound up underneath.\n\nLike I’ll be at home eating dinner and it’s not like anything is actively happening, but my body still feels like I’m in a pipeline review or waiting for something to go wrong. Yeah… I think it does, though I keep wanting to say it’s just “practical stuff,” you know?

Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t enjoy anything, because I can, or at least I try to.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Completely free? Um… I’d like to say yes, and I’m sure there are moments where I’m distracted enough that it quiets down.

But most days, no, not completely. My mind just sort of jumps around — first it’s whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, then whether someone on my team is unhappy or about to leave, then the mortgage, or a client renewal… and even a small delay can start to feel like it might turn into something major.

I know that probably sounds like a lot. I don’t mean that I’m panicking every second, just… it’s hard to find a time where there’s nothing running in the background.

I think there are definitely things that set it off, like anything around work numbers or the team… but honestly it also kind of comes up on its own sometimes.

I don’t mean that I’m panicking every second, just… it’s hard to find a time where there’s nothing running in the background.\n\nDoctor: Is your worry and anxiety present even when you're not thinking about sleep or trying to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… it’s not just at bedtime or when I’m trying to sleep.\n\nI mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days my mind sort of hops from one thing to the next — like whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, or if someone on my team is unhappy and might quit, then the mortgage, or whether a client renewal is going to come through. And then even a small delay can start feeling like it could become something much bigger.\n\nSo… yes, it’s there during the day too, not only around sleep. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is there anything in particular that you think triggers your worry and anxiety, or does it seem to come up on its own without any specific cause?\n\nPatient: I think there are definitely things that set it off, like anything around work numbers or the team… but honestly it also kind of comes up on its own sometimes.\n\nMost days my mind just jumps from, you know, are we going to hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is upset or thinking of leaving, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal. And then if there’s even a small delay, I start imagining it turning into something much bigger.\n\nI know some of that is probably normal stress, so I don’t want to overstate it, but… it feels like my brain is always finding the next thing to worry about. Does that make sense?\n\n[Last Question]\nIs your worry and anxiety focused on a specific set of concerns, like work and finances, or does it feel more general, like you're worried about everything in general?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thanks for seeing me. I guess the main thing is my sleep has just gotten really unreliable. I don’t want to make it sound overly dramatic, but even when I’m exhausted and go to bed, I end up lying there replaying work forecasts and team issues in my head. Then I’ll wake up around 3 or 4 and it’s like my brain just won’t power down again. I’m not sure if that’s the right kind of thing to come in for, but it’s been getting hard to manage.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're lying awake at night replaying work forecasts and team issues?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m panicking or anything, but I do feel worried. Most days my mind kind of jumps from one thing to another — like whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, or if someone on my team is about to quit, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal. It’s like every small delay starts to feel like it could turn into something major. I don’t know if that makes sense, but that’s usually what’s happening when I’m awake.\n\nDoctor: Is this worry about work and other things something that happens throughout the day, or is it mainly at night when you're trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: It’s… not only at night, no. I wish it were just when I’m trying to sleep, because that would feel a little more contained. But during the day, most days, my mind kind of hops from one worry to the next — like whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, then whether someone on my team might be about to quit, then the mortgage, or a client renewal.\n\nI know some of that is normal work stress, and I don’t want to overstate it, but even small delays can start feeling like they’re going to turn into something much bigger. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is the sleep problem what's been bothering you the most, or is it the worry and anxiety that's really the main issue for you?\n\nPatient: I think… if I’m being honest, it’s probably the worry more than the sleep itself. The sleep is what made me feel like I should come in, because it’s easier to point to, I guess.\n\nBut during most days my mind jumps around — like, will we hit the launch numbers, is someone on my team going to quit, what about the mortgage, or that client renewal — and then some small delay starts feeling like it could turn into something much bigger. I know that might just sound like normal responsibility, but it feels like it’s taking up a lot of space in my head.\n\nDoctor: Is this worry and anxiety mainly focused on work, or does it also affect other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly work, I think… or that’s where it starts, anyway. Like the launch numbers or whether someone on my team is unhappy, that kind of thing.\n\nBut it doesn’t really stay neatly in that box. It’ll jump to the mortgage, or a client renewal, and then I’m treating some small delay like it’s a sign something bigger is going wrong. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic, but it feels hard to shut off once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or worried even when you're not thinking about work or specific problems like the mortgage?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Maybe not “worried” in a clear, specific way all the time, but I feel kind of wound up even when nothing is actually happening.\n\nLike I can be at home eating dinner, and my body still feels like I’m sitting in a pipeline review or waiting for bad news. My shoulders stay tight a lot, even when I’m trying to relax. I don’t know if that counts as anxiety exactly, but it doesn’t feel like I fully switch off.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling wound up even when trying to relax, does that feeling of being wound up go away at any point during the day?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I could say it does. It might ease up a little if I’m distracted, but even then I still feel kind of wound up underneath.\n\nLike I’ll be at home eating dinner and it’s not like anything is actively happening, but my body still feels like I’m in a pipeline review or waiting for something to go wrong. My shoulders stay tight a lot, even when I’m trying to relax. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right, but it feels pretty constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're in situations that don't involve work or the things you mentioned like mortgages or client renewals?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it does, though I keep wanting to say it’s just “practical stuff,” you know?\n\nIt’s like my mind will start with work — whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, or whether someone on my team is unhappy or about to leave — but then it doesn’t stop there. It jumps to the mortgage, or whether a client renewal is going to fall through, and then even a small delay starts to feel like it could turn into something much bigger.\n\nSo… yes, I guess it shows up outside of work too. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but it does kind of spread.\n\nDoctor: Does your anxiety or worry tend to follow any kind of pattern or cycle throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a clean pattern exactly… it’s more like loops, if that makes sense.\n\nI’ll tell myself, “Brenda, you’ve already made the plan, you’ve done what you can, just let it go,” and I can mean that in the moment. But then five minutes later I’m back in it again, turning over some other angle. I’ll mentally check one concern off, and then three more pop up.\n\nSo I guess the cycle is… I try to reassure myself, it helps briefly, and then my mind finds the next thing. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're doing things you normally enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t enjoy anything, because I can, or at least I try to.\n\nBut even if I’m doing something nice or spending time with people, my mind can still kind of drift back. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think it’s mostly specific things, like work and money stuff… and maybe people on my team, if that counts as relationships.

But it doesn’t stay neatly in one box, if that makes sense. Most days my mind jumps from whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is about to quit, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal. And then even a small delay can start feeling like it’s going to turn into something major.

I know everyone worries about those things, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but it does feel like my brain keeps finding the next thing. Is that kind of what you mean?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thanks for seeing me. I guess the main thing is my sleep has just gotten really unreliable. I don’t want to make it sound overly dramatic, but even when I’m exhausted and go to bed, I end up lying there replaying work forecasts and team issues in my head. Then I’ll wake up around 3 or 4 and it’s like my brain just won’t power down again. I’m not sure if that’s the right kind of thing to come in for, but it’s been getting hard to manage.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're lying awake at night replaying work forecasts and team issues?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m panicking or anything, but I do feel worried. Most days my mind kind of jumps from one thing to another — like whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, or if someone on my team is about to quit, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal. It’s like every small delay starts to feel like it could turn into something major. I don’t know if that makes sense, but that’s usually what’s happening when I’m awake.\n\nDoctor: Is this worry about work and other things something that happens throughout the day, or is it mainly at night when you're trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: It’s… not only at night, no. I wish it were just when I’m trying to sleep, because that would feel a little more contained. But during the day, most days, my mind kind of hops from one worry to the next — like whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, then whether someone on my team might be about to quit, then the mortgage, or a client renewal.\n\nI know some of that is normal work stress, and I don’t want to overstate it, but even small delays can start feeling like they’re going to turn into something much bigger. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is the sleep problem what's been bothering you the most, or is it the worry and anxiety that's really the main issue for you?\n\nPatient: I think… if I’m being honest, it’s probably the worry more than the sleep itself. The sleep is what made me feel like I should come in, because it’s easier to point to, I guess.\n\nBut during most days my mind jumps around — like, will we hit the launch numbers, is someone on my team going to quit, what about the mortgage, or that client renewal — and then some small delay starts feeling like it could turn into something much bigger. I know that might just sound like normal responsibility, but it feels like it’s taking up a lot of space in my head.\n\nDoctor: Is this worry and anxiety mainly focused on work, or does it also affect other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly work, I think… or that’s where it starts, anyway. Like the launch numbers or whether someone on my team is unhappy, that kind of thing.\n\nBut it doesn’t really stay neatly in that box. It’ll jump to the mortgage, or a client renewal, and then I’m treating some small delay like it’s a sign something bigger is going wrong. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic, but it feels hard to shut off once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or worried even when you're not thinking about work or specific problems like the mortgage?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Maybe not “worried” in a clear, specific way all the time, but I feel kind of wound up even when nothing is actually happening.\n\nLike I can be at home eating dinner, and my body still feels like I’m sitting in a pipeline review or waiting for bad news. My shoulders stay tight a lot, even when I’m trying to relax. I don’t know if that counts as anxiety exactly, but it doesn’t feel like I fully switch off.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling wound up even when trying to relax, does that feeling of being wound up go away at any point during the day?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I could say it does. It might ease up a little if I’m distracted, but even then I still feel kind of wound up underneath.\n\nLike I’ll be at home eating dinner and it’s not like anything is actively happening, but my body still feels like I’m in a pipeline review or waiting for something to go wrong. My shoulders stay tight a lot, even when I’m trying to relax. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right, but it feels pretty constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're in situations that don't involve work or the things you mentioned like mortgages or client renewals?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it does, though I keep wanting to say it’s just “practical stuff,” you know?\n\nIt’s like my mind will start with work — whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, or whether someone on my team is unhappy or about to leave — but then it doesn’t stop there. It jumps to the mortgage, or whether a client renewal is going to fall through, and then even a small delay starts to feel like it could turn into something much bigger.\n\nSo… yes, I guess it shows up outside of work too. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but it does kind of spread.\n\nDoctor: Does your anxiety or worry tend to follow any kind of pattern or cycle throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a clean pattern exactly… it’s more like loops, if that makes sense.\n\nI’ll tell myself, “Brenda, you’ve already made the plan, you’ve done what you can, just let it go,” and I can mean that in the moment. But then five minutes later I’m back in it again, turning over some other angle. I’ll mentally check one concern off, and then three more pop up.\n\nSo I guess the cycle is… I try to reassure myself, it helps briefly, and then my mind finds the next thing. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're doing things you normally enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t enjoy anything, because I can, or at least I try to.\n\nBut even if I’m doing something nice or spending time with people, my mind can still kind of drift back. Like, are we going to hit the launch numbers, is someone on my team unhappy or about to quit, what if the mortgage becomes an issue, what if that client renewal doesn’t come through… and then some small delay starts feeling bigger than it probably is.\n\nSo yes, I guess it can be there even during things I’m supposed to be enjoying. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is there ever a time or situation when you feel completely free from worry or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Completely free? Um… I’d like to say yes, and I’m sure there are moments where I’m distracted enough that it quiets down.\n\nBut most days, no, not completely. My mind just sort of jumps around — first it’s whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, then whether someone on my team is unhappy or about to leave, then the mortgage, or a client renewal… and even a small delay can start to feel like it might turn into something major.\n\nI know that probably sounds like a lot. I don’t mean that I’m panicking every second, just… it’s hard to find a time where there’s nothing running in the background.\n\nDoctor: Is your worry and anxiety present even when you're not thinking about sleep or trying to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… it’s not just at bedtime or when I’m trying to sleep.\n\nI mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days my mind sort of hops from one thing to the next — like whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, or if someone on my team is unhappy and might quit, then the mortgage, or whether a client renewal is going to come through. And then even a small delay can start feeling like it could become something much bigger.\n\nSo… yes, it’s there during the day too, not only around sleep. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is there anything in particular that you think triggers your worry and anxiety, or does it seem to come up on its own without any specific cause?\n\nPatient: I think there are definitely things that set it off, like anything around work numbers or the team… but honestly it also kind of comes up on its own sometimes.\n\nMost days my mind just jumps from, you know, are we going to hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is upset or thinking of leaving, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal. And then if there’s even a small delay, I start imagining it turning into something much bigger.\n\nI know some of that is probably normal stress, so I don’t want to overstate it, but… it feels like my brain is always finding the next thing to worry about. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your worry and anxiety focused on a specific set of concerns, like work and finances, or does it feel more general, like you're worried about everything in general?\n\nPatient: It’s probably more centered on work and finances, I guess… at least those are the things I can name most easily.\n\nLike, my mind goes from whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is unhappy or might quit, then it’s the mortgage, or a client renewal. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your worry and anxiety mainly about specific things like work, finances, and relationships, or does it feel like you're worried about almost everything in general?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly specific things, like work and money stuff… and maybe people on my team, if that counts as relationships.\n\nBut it doesn’t stay neatly in one box, if that makes sense. I wish it were just when I’m trying to sleep, because that would feel a little more contained. But during the day, most days, my mind kind of hops from one worry to the next — like whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, then whether someone on my team might be about to quit, then the mortgage, or a client renewal.\n\nI know some of that is normal work stress, and I don’t want to overstate it, but even small delays can start feeling like they’re going to turn into something much bigger. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is the sleep problem what's been bothering you the most, or is it the worry and anxiety that's really the main issue for you?\n\nPatient: I think… if I’m being honest, it’s probably the worry more than the sleep itself. The sleep is what made me feel like I should come in, because it’s easier to point to, I guess.\n\nBut during most days my mind jumps around — like, will we hit the launch numbers, is someone on my team going to quit, what about the mortgage, or that client renewal — and then some small delay starts feeling like it could turn into something much bigger. I know that might just sound like normal responsibility, but it feels like it’s taking up a lot of space in my head.\n\nDoctor: Is this worry and anxiety mainly focused on work, or does it also affect other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly work, I think… or that’s where it starts, anyway. Like the launch numbers or whether someone on my team is unhappy, that kind of thing.\n\nBut it doesn’t really stay neatly in that box. It’ll jump to the mortgage, or a client renewal, and then I’m treating some small delay like it’s a sign something bigger is going wrong. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic, but it feels hard to shut off once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or worried even when you're not thinking about work or specific problems like the mortgage?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Maybe not “worried” in a clear, specific way all the time, but I feel kind of wound up even when nothing is actually happening.\n\nLike I can be at home eating dinner, and my body still feels like I’m sitting in a pipeline review or waiting for bad news. My shoulders stay tight a lot, even when I’m trying to relax. I don’t know if that counts as anxiety exactly, but it doesn’t feel like I fully switch off.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling wound up even when trying to relax, does that feeling of being wound up go away at any point during the day?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I could say it does. It might ease up a little if I’m distracted, but even then I still feel kind of wound up underneath.\n\nLike I’ll be at home eating dinner and it’s not like anything is actively happening, but my body still feels like I’m in a pipeline review or waiting for something to go wrong. My shoulders stay tight a lot, even when I’m trying to relax. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right, but it feels pretty constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're in situations that don't involve work or the things you mentioned like mortgages or client renewals?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it does, though I keep wanting to say it’s just “practical stuff,” you know?\n\nIt’s like my mind will start with work — whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, or whether someone on my team is unhappy or about to leave — but then it doesn’t stop there. It jumps to the mortgage, or whether a client renewal is going to fall through, and then even a small delay starts to feel like it could turn into something much bigger.\n\nSo… yes, I guess it shows up outside of work too. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but it does kind of spread.\n\nDoctor: Does your anxiety or worry tend to follow any kind of pattern or cycle throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a clean pattern exactly… it’s more like loops, if that makes sense.\n\nI’ll tell myself, “Brenda, you’ve already made the plan, you’ve done what you can, just let it go,” and I can mean that in the moment. But then five minutes later I’m back in it again, turning over some other angle. I’ll mentally check one concern off, and then three more pop up.\n\nSo I guess the cycle is… I try to reassure myself, it helps briefly, and then my mind finds the next thing. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're doing things you normally enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t enjoy anything, because I can, or at least I try to.\n\nBut even if I’m doing something nice or spending time with people, my mind can still kind of drift back. Like, are we going to hit the launch numbers, is someone on my team unhappy or about to quit, what if the mortgage becomes an issue, what if that client renewal doesn’t come through… and then some small delay starts feeling bigger than it probably is.\n\nSo yes, I guess it can be there even during things I’m supposed to be enjoying. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is there ever a time or situation when you feel completely free from worry or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Completely free? Um… I’d like to say yes, and I’m sure there are moments where I’m distracted enough that it quiets down.\n\nBut most days, no, not completely. My mind just sort of jumps around — first it’s whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, then whether someone on my team is unhappy or about to leave, then the mortgage, or a client renewal… and even a small delay can start to feel like it might turn into something major.\n\nI know that probably sounds like a lot. I don’t mean that I’m panicking every second, just… it’s hard to find a time where there’s nothing running in the background.\n\nDoctor: Is your worry and anxiety present even when you're not thinking about sleep or trying to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… it’s not just at bedtime or when I’m trying to sleep.\n\nI mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days my mind sort of hops from one thing to the next — like whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, or if someone on my team is unhappy and might quit, then the mortgage, or whether a client renewal is going to come through. And then even a small delay can start feeling like it could become something much bigger.\n\nSo… yes, it’s there during the day too, not only around sleep. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is there anything in particular that you think triggers your worry and anxiety, or does it seem to come up on its own without any specific cause?\n\nPatient: I think there are definitely things that set it off, like anything around work numbers or the team… but honestly it also kind of comes up on its own sometimes.\n\nMost days my mind just jumps from, you know, are we going to hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is upset or thinking of leaving, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal. And then if there’s even a small delay, I start imagining it turning into something much bigger.\n\nI know some of that is probably normal stress, so I don’t want to overstate it, but… it feels like my brain is always finding the next thing to worry about. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your worry and anxiety focused on a specific set of concerns, like work and finances, or does it feel more general, like you're worried about everything in general?\n\nPatient: It’s probably more centered on work and finances, I guess… at least those are the things I can name most easily.\n\nLike, my mind goes from whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is unhappy or might quit, then it’s the mortgage, or a client renewal. And sometimes even a small delay starts feeling like it could turn into something really major.\n\nI don’t think I’m worried about literally everything, but it does feel like my brain can turn a lot of things into a problem pretty quickly. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your worry and anxiety mainly about specific things like work, finances, and relationships, or does it feel like you're worried about almost everything in general?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly specific things, like work and money stuff… and maybe people on my team, if that counts as relationships.\n\nBut it doesn’t stay neatly in one box, if that makes sense. Most days my mind jumps from whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is about to quit, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal. And then even a small delay can start feeling like it’s going to turn into something major.\n\nI know everyone worries about those things, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but it does feel like my brain keeps finding the next thing. Is that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worry and anxiety could be described as being about almost everything in general, or is it mostly focused on specific areas like work and finances?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s mostly focused on specific areas, like work and finances… though it can feel pretty broad once it starts.\n\nMost days, my mind jumps from whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is about to quit, then to the mortgage or a client renewal. I don’t want to make it sound overly dramatic, but even when I’m exhausted and go to bed, I end up lying there replaying work forecasts and team issues in my head. Then I’ll wake up around 3 or 4 and it’s like my brain just won’t power down again. I’m not sure if that’s the right kind of thing to come in for, but it’s been getting hard to manage.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're lying awake at night replaying work forecasts and team issues?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m panicking or anything, but I do feel worried. Most days my mind kind of jumps from one thing to another — like whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, or if someone on my team is about to quit, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal. It’s like every small delay starts to feel like it could turn into something major. I don’t know if that makes sense, but that’s usually what’s happening when I’m awake.\n\nDoctor: Is this worry about work and other things something that happens throughout the day, or is it mainly at night when you're trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: It’s… not only at night, no. I wish it were just when I’m trying to sleep, because that would feel a little more contained. But during the day, most days, my mind kind of hops from one worry to the next — like whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, then whether someone on my team might be about to quit, then the mortgage, or a client renewal.\n\nI know some of that is normal work stress, and I don’t want to overstate it, but even small delays can start feeling like they’re going to turn into something much bigger. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is the sleep problem what's been bothering you the most, or is it the worry and anxiety that's really the main issue for you?\n\nPatient: I think… if I’m being honest, it’s probably the worry more than the sleep itself. The sleep is what made me feel like I should come in, because it’s easier to point to, I guess.\n\nBut during most days my mind jumps around — like, will we hit the launch numbers, is someone on my team going to quit, what about the mortgage, or that client renewal — and then some small delay starts feeling like it could turn into something much bigger. I know that might just sound like normal responsibility, but it feels like it’s taking up a lot of space in my head.\n\nDoctor: Is this worry and anxiety mainly focused on work, or does it also affect other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly work, I think… or that’s where it starts, anyway. Like the launch numbers or whether someone on my team is unhappy, that kind of thing.\n\nBut it doesn’t really stay neatly in that box. It’ll jump to the mortgage, or a client renewal, and then I’m treating some small delay like it’s a sign something bigger is going wrong. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic, but it feels hard to shut off once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or worried even when you're not thinking about work or specific problems like the mortgage?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Maybe not “worried” in a clear, specific way all the time, but I feel kind of wound up even when nothing is actually happening.\n\nLike I can be at home eating dinner, and my body still feels like I’m sitting in a pipeline review or waiting for bad news. My shoulders stay tight a lot, even when I’m trying to relax. I don’t know if that counts as anxiety exactly, but it doesn’t feel like I fully switch off.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling wound up even when trying to relax, does that feeling of being wound up go away at any point during the day?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I could say it does. It might ease up a little if I’m distracted, but even then I still feel kind of wound up underneath.\n\nLike I’ll be at home eating dinner and it’s not like anything is actively happening, but my body still feels like I’m in a pipeline review or waiting for something to go wrong. My shoulders stay tight a lot, even when I’m trying to relax. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right, but it feels pretty constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're in situations that don't involve work or the things you mentioned like mortgages or client renewals?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it does, though I keep wanting to say it’s just “practical stuff,” you know?\n\nIt’s like my mind will start with work — whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, or whether someone on my team is unhappy or about to leave — but then it doesn’t stop there. It jumps to the mortgage, or whether a client renewal is going to fall through, and then even a small delay starts to feel like it could turn into something much bigger.\n\nSo… yes, I guess it shows up outside of work too. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but it does kind of spread.\n\nDoctor: Does your anxiety or worry tend to follow any kind of pattern or cycle throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a clean pattern exactly… it’s more like loops, if that makes sense.\n\nI’ll tell myself, “Brenda, you’ve already made the plan, you’ve done what you can, just let it go,” and I can mean that in the moment. But then five minutes later I’m back in it again, turning over some other angle. I’ll mentally check one concern off, and then three more pop up.\n\nSo I guess the cycle is… I try to reassure myself, it helps briefly, and then my mind finds the next thing. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're doing things you normally enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t enjoy anything, because I can, or at least I try to.\n\nBut even if I’m doing something nice or spending time with people, my mind can still kind of drift back. Like, are we going to hit the launch numbers, is someone on my team unhappy or about to quit, what if the mortgage becomes an issue, what if that client renewal doesn’t come through… and then some small delay starts feeling bigger than it probably is.\n\nSo yes, I guess it can be there even during things I’m supposed to be enjoying. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is there ever a time or situation when you feel completely free from worry or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Completely free? Um… I’d like to say yes, and I’m sure there are moments where I’m distracted enough that it quiets down.\n\nBut most days, no, not completely. My mind just sort of jumps around — first it’s whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, then whether someone on my team is unhappy or about to leave, then the mortgage, or a client renewal… and even a small delay can start to feel like it might turn into something major.\n\nI know that probably sounds like a lot. I don’t mean that I’m panicking every second, just… it’s hard to find a time where there’s nothing running in the background.\n\nDoctor: Is your worry and anxiety present even when you're not thinking about sleep or trying to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… it’s not just at bedtime or when I’m trying to sleep.\n\nI mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days my mind sort of hops from one thing to the next — like whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, or if someone on my team is unhappy and might quit, then the mortgage, or whether a client renewal is going to come through. And then even a small delay can start feeling like it could become something much bigger.\n\nSo… yes, it’s there during the day too, not only around sleep. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is there anything in particular that you think triggers your worry and anxiety, or does it seem to come up on its own without any specific cause?\n\nPatient: I think there are definitely things that set it off, like anything around work numbers or the team… but honestly it also kind of comes up on its own sometimes.\n\nMost days my mind just jumps from, you know, are we going to hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is upset or thinking of leaving, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal. And then if there’s even a small delay, I start imagining it turning into something much bigger.\n\nI know some of that is probably normal stress, so I don’t want to overstate it, but… it feels like my brain is always finding the next thing to worry about. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your worry and anxiety focused on a specific set of concerns, like work and finances, or does it feel more general, like you're worried about everything in general?\n\nPatient: It’s probably more centered on work and finances, I guess… at least those are the things I can name most easily.\n\nLike, my mind goes from whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is unhappy or might quit, then it’s the mortgage, or a client renewal. I can relax a little, especially if I’m doing something calming, but… completely relax? Probably not, if I’m being honest.

But then five minutes later I’m back in it again, turning over some other angle. I’ll mentally check one concern off, and then three more pop up.\n\nSo I guess the cycle is… I try to reassure myself, it helps briefly, and then my mind finds the next thing. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're doing things you normally enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is there ever a time or situation when you feel completely free from worry or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Completely free?
